-topoi. Thus it would suffice to prove the analog of the above proposition taking the internal hom

\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(\mathbb{E}_{n},L_{K(n)}\pi^{!}\mathbb{S}_{\widehat{p}})

in
v
-sheaves instead. For that it suffices to show that the internal hom in v-sheaves already lies in the
K(n)
-local etale full subcategory (as then it will a fortiori identify with the internal Hom in
K(n)
-local etale shaves). As by definition etale sheaves are detected on pullback to any cover, and
e^{\ast}\pi^{!}\mathbb{S}_{\widehat{p}}
is a shift of
L_{K(n)}\mathbb{S}
, it suffices to show the following.

Lemma 14.2
.

The internal hom in light condensed spectra

\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(E_{n},L_{K(n)}\mathbb{S})

is
K(n)
-locally etale, i.e. it is discrete mod
(p,v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-1})
.

Proof.

As
L_{K(n)}\mathbb{S}=\pi_{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{n}
(
12.14
), we have

\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(E_{n},L_{K(n)}\mathbb{S})\simeq\pi_{\ast}\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(E_{n},\mathbb{E}_{n}).

Now, consider the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module (in
K(n)
-local
v
-sheaves on
\ast/\mathbb{G}_{n}
) given by
L_{K(n)}(\mathbb{E}_{n}\otimes E_{n})
. By the Galois property
12.13
, we have

L_{K(n)}(\mathbb{E}_{n}\otimes E_{n})\simeq L_{K(n)}(\mathbb{E}_{n}\otimes e_{\ast}\mathbb{S}).

as
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-modules. Since
\mathbb{E}_{n}
is a
K(n)
-local
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-module, it follows that

\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(E_{n},\mathbb{E}_{n})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(e_{\ast}\mathbb{S},\mathbb{E}_{n}).

Now we use the solid formalism. Since
\mathbb{E}_{n}
is solid, this internal hom in
v
-sheaves is also solid. Consider the induced natural map

\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(e_{\ast}\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S})\otimes^{\square}\mathbb{E}_{n}\rightarrow\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(e_{\ast}\mathbb{S},\mathbb{E}_{n})

We claim this is an isomorphism. Indeed we can check on underlying solid spectra (i.e. after pullback to
\ast
), and then the claim is given by
13.10
. (Once again, this is where we use a special feature of Morava E-theory as compared to a general Galois extension.) Now by definition of solidification
\underline{\operatorname{hom}}_{v}(e_{\ast}\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S})
identifies with the solid sheaf on
\ast/G
corresponding to
\mathbb{S}[G]^{\square}
viewed as a left module over itself. Then in Remark
13.28
we already calculated that
\pi_{\ast}(\mathbb{S}[G]^{\square}\otimes^{\square}\mathbb{E}_{n})
is
K(n)
-locally etale, finishing the proof.
∎

Combining these two lemmas, we get the following, giving the equivariant generalization of Hopkins’ result (already obtained by
[
Bea+22
]
in different language).

Theorem 14.3
.

Let
p
be a prime and
n\geq 1
. There is a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{E}_{n}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{map}}_{v}(\mathbb{E}_{n},L_{K(n)}\pi^{!}(\mathbb{S}_{\widehat{p}})),

where
\underline{\operatorname{map}}_{v}
denotes the internal mapping spectrum in
\operatorname{Sh}_{v}(\ast/\mathbb{G}_{n};\operatorname{Sp})
.

Proof.

By the first lemma, we get the analog of this with
v
replaced by
et
. By the second lemma, the two different notions of internal mapping object agree.
∎

Remark 14.4
.

In
[
Bea+22
]
, this result is fruitfully used to understand self-duality properties of spectra of the form
(E_{n})^{hG}
, for
G\subset\mathbb{G}_{n}
a suitable finite subgroup. For their analysis, one needs to get a good handle on the dualizing object
\pi^{!}(\mathbb{S}_{\widehat{p}})
. A first step is the
linearization
of this problem, provided by our main theorem
10.14
(which they independently showed holds upon restriction to suitable finite subgroups). As a second step, they transfer from the
p
-adic J-homomorphism to the real J-homomorphism using an integral structure on the adjoint representation, again restricted to a suitable finite subgroup. This is accomplished using the reciprocity law from
[
Cla12
]
or Section
11
(which they reprove a special case of). In the third step, they use
orientation
properties of
E_{n}
with respect to the real J-homomorphism in conjunction with some very nice analysis of characteristic classes of representations to pin down the relevant twists.

It would be interesting to understand what kind of orientation properties one has directly for the
p
-adic J-homomorphism, without going over to the real
J
-homomorphism.
